### 🔴 `T478` — the register, and the shape of how it was missed

`p749` took **print disability**, `p753` took the **Deaf estate**, `p750` took **wellbeing**, `p752` took the **exit** to employment. Every one of those registers serves a learner who is **already enrolled in schooling**. The register below serves the years **before** that — and it is a different sector with different buyers (a municipality, not a faculty), different staff (an early-years educator or a community health worker, not a lecturer), different instruments and different law. Mention counts across all eight live shelves, measured `2026-11-10`:

| Term | Mentions across `753` passes | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `childcare` / `daycare` / `nursery` | 🔴 **`0` / `0` / `0`** | The three words the sector trades under. |
| `developmental screening` | 🔴 **`0`** | The clinical function that routes a child to support. |
| `ASQ` / `ECERS` | 🔴 **`0` / `0`** | The dominant screening and quality-rating instruments. |
| `parent engagement` | 🔴 **`0`** | The primary delivery channel before enrolment. |
| `anganwadi` | 🔴 **`0`** | India's `~1.37M`-centre public pre-primary network. |
| `foundational learning` / `under-five` | 🔴 **`0` / `0`** | The sector's own term of art, and its age band. |
| `preschool` / `pre-k` / `kindergarten` | 🔴 **`3` / `3` / `3`** | Incidental mentions, no rows. |
| `head start` | 🔴 **`1`** | The largest US federal ECEC programme. |
| `early childhood` / `montessori` / `early literacy` | 🟡 **`7` / `7` / `7`** | Prose only. |
| `ECEC` / `numeracy` | 🟡 **`8` / `8`** | Prose only. |

🟢 **The lesson generalises `p752`'s and `p753`'s.** This base has repeatedly discovered that a word it treats as one register — "accessibility", "employability" — is several. **"Education" is the same word, and ECEC is the part of it this base had silently scoped out:** every shelf heading says *education*, and every row underneath assumed a student with a timetable.

### 🔴 `T485` — the row-level rule for this entire register: the code is open, the instrument is not

This is the register's defining licence structure and it does not appear anywhere else on this base. In ECEC the valuable asset is a **psychometric instrument** — a validated question set with norms — and the dominant instruments are **commercial copyrighted products**: ASQ-3 (Brookes), Denver II (Denver Developmental Materials, Inc.), M-CHAT-R/F. **An MIT licence on a screening app does not convey any right to the instrument inside it.**

🟢 **The honest projects in this register already know this and show the compliant pattern**, which is why `mychild-app` and `tumbuh-kembang` are on the shelf despite low star counts:

- `mychild-app` **substitutes** a public-domain bank (CDC-derived milestones) and says so.
- `tumbuh-kembang` **carves the dataset out of its own MIT grant** and names the rights-holder it is not affiliated with.

🔴 **Both are telling you the same thing: in an ECEC engagement the instrument is a licensing work package with a budget, or it is the client's own validated content.** Never a scrape. `P271` is written around this constraint.

## 🆕 `p753` — 🔴 **THE DEAF-LEARNER AND SIGN-LANGUAGE ACCESS LAYER: `p749` TOOK THE PRINT-DISABILITY ESTATE AND LEFT THE DEAF ESTATE AT NEAR-NIL — `vlibras` `0`, `signwriting` `0`, `sign language processing` `0`, `WLASL` `1`. 🔴 THE WORLD'S LEADING OPEN SIGN-LANGUAGE STACK CHANGED OWNER IN 2026 AND ITS LICENCE POSTURE SPLIT THREE WAYS INSIDE ONE ORGANISATION. 🔴 THE `800★` FLAGSHIP IS NOW DUAL-TIER: FREE FOR THE CLIENT, PAID FOR GLOBANT. 🟢 THE ONE REGION WHERE THE STATE SHIPS THE STACK IS LATAM.**

**`6` agent rows (`4` MIT, `1` LGPL-3.0, `1` GPL-3.0 — the copyleft rows are the register, not an oversight) · `35` addresses probed · `24` licence payloads byte-read · `3` index values materially corrected, **all `3` in the refusing direction** · `10` compliant grant-absences recorded as `T302` honest negatives · `16` query denominators plus `2` licence-distribution samples measured · `T465`–`T477` declared.**

### 🔴 `T465` — the register, and why `752` passes missed it

`p749` (`2026-11-10`) took the **alternative-format estate** — DAISY, `liblouis`, `readium`, `epubcheck` — and did it thoroughly. That estate serves **print disability**. The estate that serves **Deaf and hard-of-hearing learners** is a different technology stack with different publishers, different licences and different law, and this base held almost none of it. Mention counts across all eight live shelves, measured `2026-11-10`:

| Term | Mentions across `752` passes | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `vlibras` | 🔴 **`0`** | The Brazilian federal government's sign-language suite. |
| `signwriting` | 🔴 **`0`** | The written notation the translation stack is built on. |
| `pose-format` / `sign language processing` | 🔴 **`0`** | The field's substrate and its reference organisation. |
| `montreal forced` / `common voice` | 🔴 **`0`** | — |
| `WLASL` | 🔴 **`1`** | The most-cited ASL dataset in the literature. |
| `sign.mt` | 🔴 **`1`** | The flagship application. |
| `Deaf` | 🔴 **`7`** | The population the register exists for. |
| `sign language` (any) | `~30` | Almost all of it one already-held LATAM row (`alfalibras`). |

🟢 **The one thing the base did hold was right:** [`TechnoDark-ti/alfalibras`](https://github.com/TechnoDark-ti/alfalibras) — a Libras row placed in Brazil. **It was a single application sitting over a substrate this base had never opened.**

### 🔴 `T466` — the finding that defines the register: the supply runs in the wrong direction

Measured against `gh api -X GET search/repositories` `total_count`, `2026-11-10`:

| Direction | Query | Repositories |
|---|---|---|
| **sign → text** (recognising what a signer says) | `sign language recognition` | 🟢 **`10,467`** |
| **sign → text** | `sign language translation` | 🟢 **`4,980`** |
| **text → sign** (producing a signed rendering) | `sign language avatar synthesis` | 🔴 **`6`** |
| **text → sign** | `sign language production avatar` | 🔴 **`4`** — and `3 / 4` are not sign-language repositories at all (`T467`) |

🔴 **Education needs the direction that does not exist.** A Deaf learner in a classroom is overwhelmingly a *receiver* of institutional content — the lecture, the courseware, the assessment rubric, the safeguarding notice — and rendering that content into a signed track is **text → sign**. The open-source field has spent its effort on **sign → text**, which is the direction a *hearing* institution wants in order to read a Deaf person's input. The ratio is roughly **`2,600 : 1`** (`10,467` against `4`).

🟢 **The consequence for a proposal, stated plainly:** in this register Globant does not shop for a recognition model — there are ten thousand of them and they are mostly coursework. **It assembles a production pipeline**, and the whole permissive supply for that pipeline is `~6` repositories in one organisation plus one government suite.

### 🔴 `T467` — `sign language mcp server` → `12` is really `0`, and this is the third reproduction

`p751`'s `T444` and `p752`'s `T456` found that a short vertical token over-reports when it collides with a common stem. Here the collision is the English word **sign** (signature, signed, signing):

| Returned for `sign language mcp server` (`12` total) | What it actually is |
|---|---|
| `DottedSign-Official/dottedsign-mcp` (`142★`) | 🔴 An **e-signature** MCP server. |
| `LGDiMaggio/mcp-motor-current-signature-analysis` (`9★`) | 🔴 Motor current **signature** analysis. |
| `SuperdeMan/drone-agent`, `pratikkp24/shio` | 🔴 **signed** approvals / policy language. |
| `Soflutionltd/MemoryPilot`, `Bitget-AI/agent-mcp`, … | 🔴 Unrelated. |

🔴 **`0 / 12` are sign-language artefacts. `sign language agent llm` → `14` is the same: `0` genuine hits** (an "AI slop" detector, a traffic-**sign** agent, a Spotify CLI). **There is no MCP server and no agent framework for sign language in open source as of `2026-11-10`.** That is a build opportunity, not a procurement one, and it is the clearest nil this base has measured in a register with a `10,467`-repository head.

### 🔴 `T468` — the ownership event: the field's reference stack is now owned by a US for-profit, and the bytes prove it

The `sign-language-processing` organisation (`39` repositories) is the academic reference stack of this field — Zurich NLP lineage, the `.pose` format, the SignWriting tooling, the field's documentation site. **Its copyright holder has changed, and the licence payloads carry the evidence as two distinct byte bands:**

| Payload band | Bytes | sha256₁₂ | Holder line | Repositories |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Older MIT | `1,081 B` | `e4375b39719a` | `Copyright (c) 2024 Sign Language Processing` | `signwriting`, `segmentation`, `signwriting-translation`, `fluent-pose-synthesis`, `mediapipe-hand-crop-fix` |
| Older MIT | `1,071 B` | `1c821db6b337` | `Copyright (c) 2021 Amit Moryossef` | `pose` |
| 🔴 **Newer MIT** | `1,068 B` | `04a767716fd5` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2026 Nagish Inc.`** | `pose-evaluation`, `3d-hands-benchmark` |
| 🔴 **Newer CC BY-NC** | `19,301 B` | `e8bb0d05c06f` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2026 Nagish Inc.`** | `pose-to-video`, `signbank-plus` |
| 🔴 **Newer CC BY** | `18,689 B` | `59726ab13774` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2026 Nagish Inc.`** | `sign-language-processing.github.io` |
| 🔴 **Dual-tier** | `17,404 B` | `82908ae3f300` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2022 Nagish Inc.`** | `sign/translate` — now **`Rylo Translate`** at `rylo.com` |

🔴 **The flagship was renamed.** [`sign/translate`](https://github.com/sign/translate) — `800★`, the application the whole field points at — opens its licence with *"License for `Rylo Translate` (https://rylo.com/sign/translate)"*. **Nagish Inc. is a US for-profit accessibility company.** The research artefacts are still public; the *terms* are now a commercial product's terms.

🟢 **Why this is the finding and not a footnote:** a pass that read the index would see `MIT`, `MIT`, `NOASSERTION`, `MIT` and conclude the organisation is permissive. **Byte-read, the same organisation is permissive for the libraries, NonCommercial for the data and the renderer, and paid for the application.** Placement: **North America** on the holder line, **EMEA** on the research lineage — both are true and a proposal needs the first one.

### 🔴 `T469` — the fault line runs through the pipeline, not around it

The one end-to-end **text → sign** pipeline in open source is [`spoken-to-signed-translation`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/spoken-to-signed-translation) — `105★`, **MIT**, `Copyright (c) 2026 ZurichNLP`, pushed `2026-10-18`. It is text → gloss → **pose** → video. Stage by stage, byte-read:

| Stage | Component | Grant — **byte-read `2026-11-10`** | Usable in a paid Globant deliverable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sensing | [`google-ai-edge/mediapipe`](https://github.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe) `37,636★` | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** · `12,331 B` · `8707eef05339` | 🟢 Yes |
| Pose container | [`pose`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/pose) `116★` | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,071 B` · `1c821db6b337` | 🟢 Yes |
| Text → gloss → pose | [`spoken-to-signed-translation`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/spoken-to-signed-translation) `105★` | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,066 B` · `79c5160cf846` | 🟢 Yes |
| Segmentation | [`segmentation`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/segmentation) `19★` | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,081 B` · `e4375b39719a` | 🟢 Yes |
| Prosody / fluency | [`fluent-pose-synthesis`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/fluent-pose-synthesis) `14★` | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,081 B` · `e4375b39719a` | 🟢 Yes |
| Evaluation | [`pose-evaluation`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/pose-evaluation) `11★` | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,068 B` · `04a767716fd5` | 🟢 Yes |
| 🔴 **Pose → photorealistic video** | [`pose-to-video`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/pose-to-video) `19★` | 🔴 **CC BY-NC 4.0** · `19,301 B` · `e8bb0d05c06f` | 🔴 **No** |
| 🔴 **Text → pose model** | [`transcription`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/transcription) `50★` | 🔴 **No grant at `12` probed paths** | 🔴 **No** |
| 🔴 **Training data** | [`signbank-plus`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/signbank-plus) `23★` | 🔴 **CC BY-NC 4.0** · byte-identical to `pose-to-video` | 🔴 **No** |

🟢 **The rule, and it is the commercially useful sentence of this pass:** *everything up to and including the pose is permissive; the moment the pipeline turns a pose into a photorealistic human, the grant becomes NonCommercial.* An engagement that ships an **avatar** rendering of the pose is clear; one that ships **photorealistic video** of a signer is not, and the substitution is an engineering decision made at design time, not a licence question discovered at delivery.

### 🔴 `T470` — the `800★` refusal, stated the way a proposal has to state it

[`sign/translate`](https://github.com/sign/translate) — **`800★`**, index `NOASSERTION`, licence at `LICENSE.md`, `17,404 B`, sha256₁₂ `82908ae3f300` — is **two licences in one file**:

> *"The license for `Rylo Translate` is divided into two tiers, based on the type of your legal entity. **Individuals, non-profit organizations, and educational institutions are permitted to use `Rylo Translate` for sign language translation without charge, while a separate license is required for for-profit commercial organizations.**"*

- 🟢 **Free tier:** **CC BY-NC-SA 4.0**, `Copyright (c) 2022 Nagish Inc.` — Attribution, **NonCommercial**, **ShareAlike** on adaptations. *"Patent and trademark rights are not licensed under this Public License."*
- 🔴 **Commercial tier:** required for any entity that does not meet the free criteria; *"the scope of its usage and the licensing fee will be provided upon request"* (`research@rylo.com`).

🔴 **The distinction a client engagement turns on, and it is unusual enough to be worth a slide:** **the university can run this for free; Globant cannot bill for building on it.** An engagement can legitimately (a) advise the institution to deploy it under the free tier with Globant doing no derivative work, or (b) obtain the commercial licence with a quoted fee, or (c) assemble the MIT pipeline in `T469` instead. 🟢 **Option (c) is the one this base recommends, because it is the one whose cost is known today.**

### 🔴 `T474` — the two existence oracles disagree exactly on renamed addresses, and `raw` is the permissive one

Both oracles were re-controlled this pass and `p752`'s `T455` holds — `repo:{owner}/{name}` *is* valid on `search/repositories`. The new finding is what their **disagreement** means:

| Address | `raw…/HEAD/README.md` | `repo:` `total_count` | Truth |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ai-forever/slovo` | 🟢 **`200`** | 🔴 **`0`** | 🔴 **Renamed.** The canonical address is [`hukenovs/slovo`](https://github.com/hukenovs/slovo) (`98★`) — found by a keyword query, not by the probe. |
| `sign-language-processing/pose-format` | 🔴 `404` | 🔴 `0` | 🔴 **Gone.** The library now lives at [`sign-language-processing/pose`](https://github.com/sign-language-processing/pose) (`116★`, MIT) while the **package** is still named `pose-format`. |
| `sign/translate` | 🟢 `200` | 🟢 `800★` | 🟢 Alive at its own address — and renamed *inside* (`T468`). |

🟢 **The rule to add to the probe chain:** *a `raw` `200` proves bytes are served, not that the address is canonical — GitHub serves renamed repositories through a redirect.* **`repo:` `total_count` `0` against a `raw` `200` is the rename signal**, and the response is to find the new owner by keyword before writing the row. A pass that trusted `raw` alone would have published `ai-forever/slovo` as a live address; a pass that trusted `repo:` alone would have published `pose-format`'s successor as nonexistent.

> **Instrument note — the mandated control re-ran and still has no discriminating power.** `curl -sI https://github.com/{owner}/{repo}` returned the proxy's `HTTP/1.1 200 Connection Established` **followed by `403`** on an address proven alive (`ai-forever/slovo`). ⚠️ **`p746`'s trap reproduced exactly: the first line reads like success and is the proxy's, not GitHub's.** The working probe remains `raw.githubusercontent.com/{owner}/{repo}/HEAD/README.md`, re-controlled `2 / 2` this pass (`200` alive / `404` on an invented address), now with the `T474` rename caveat attached. 🔴 **New channel limit recorded:** `WebFetch` resolved **`0 / 3`** non-allowlisted hosts this pass (`federalregister.gov`, `upcea.edu`, `audioeye.com` → `ENOTFOUND`), so `T475`'s regulatory correction is corroborated across independent secondary sources and **declared as not primary-verified**.

## 🆕 `p752` — 🟢 **THE EDUCATION-TO-EMPLOYMENT TRANSITION LAYER: `p681` CALLED THIS SUPPLY "POOR AND ALL DORMANT" AND PLACED IT EMEA-ONLY. 🟢 THE DORMANT TRIO IS STILL DORMANT — AND THE LIVE SUCCESSORS WERE PUSHED THE DAY OF THIS PASS. 🔴 THE NORTH AMERICA COUNTERPART WAS AT `0` MENTIONS ACROSS `751` PASSES AND ITS INDEX HID AN APACHE-2.0 GRANT. 🔴 A `371,756★` REPOSITORY IS PERSONAL-USE-ONLY.**

**`6` agent rows (`5` MIT, `1` Apache-2.0, all byte-read) · `38` addresses probed · `19` licence payloads byte-read · `2` index values materially corrected · `21` query denominators measured · `T453`–`T464` declared.**

### 🟢 `T453` — the finding that defines the pass: a verdict went stale and nobody re-measured it

`p681` (`2026-11-06`) placed the skills register on [`../intel/market.md`](../intel/market.md) under **EMEA** and wrote: *"the measured supply behind it is poor and the gap is the engagement: three permissive ESCO projects were probed and **all three are dormant**."* This pass re-measured those three addresses and **`p681` was correct about every one of them**:

| `p681`'s dormant trio | Stars | Grant | Last push — **re-measured `2026-11-10`** | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [`nestauk/skills-taxonomy-v2`](https://github.com/nestauk/skills-taxonomy-v2) | `43★` | MIT | `2022-10-12` | 🔴 Still dormant — **4 years cold**. |
| [`tabiya-tech/tabiya-open-dataset`](https://github.com/tabiya-tech/tabiya-open-dataset) | `7★` | 🔴 `NONE` | `2024-03-06` | 🔴 Still dormant, still no grant. |
| [`tabiya-tech/tabiya-esco-datasets-and-tools`](https://github.com/tabiya-tech/tabiya-esco-datasets-and-tools) | `8★` | MIT | `2024-08-29` | 🔴 Still dormant — the row this base already holds. |

🔴 **The defect is not in the measurement; it is in the conclusion drawn from it.** `p681` probed the *datasets* and inferred the *register* was poor. Measured against the same publishers on the same day, the register is not poor — it is **actively maintained, and the base was holding the wrong repository from each publisher**:

| What `p681` held | What it should have held | Stars | Grant | Last push |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nestauk/skills-taxonomy-v2` — `43★`, dead `2022-10` | [`nestauk/ojd_daps_skills`](https://github.com/nestauk/ojd_daps_skills) — **the successor from the same publisher** | 🟢 **`162★`** | MIT | `2025-06-09` |
| `tabiya-tech/tabiya-esco-datasets-and-tools` — the *data dump* | [`tabiya-tech/taxonomy-model-application`](https://github.com/tabiya-tech/taxonomy-model-application) — **the running platform** | `20★` | MIT | 🟢 **`2026-11-10` — the day of this pass** |
| — nothing | [`tabiya-tech/compass`](https://github.com/tabiya-tech/compass) — **the conversational agent itself** | `14★` | MIT | 🟢 **`2026-11-10` — the day of this pass** |

🟢 **The rule this yields, and it generalises past this register:** *a dormancy verdict is a statement about an address, never about a register.* `p681`'s three probes were all **data** repositories; the **code** repositories beside them in the same organisations are among the most actively maintained artefacts in this entire base. Before quoting a scarcity verdict to a client, re-probe the publisher, not the repository.

### 🔴 `T456` — `esco mcp server` matches a different word, and the pollution is Romance-language

`5` of the `7` results match **`esco-`/`escola`/`escolar`** as a *substring* — `escouade-io/escouade-mail-mcp` (a mail plugin), `andreferrazz/escoladobreno-mcp` and `josesaid/control-escolar-spring-ai-mcp` (Portuguese/Spanish *school*). 🔵 **This is the `T444` shape from `p751` reproduced in a second register:** a four-letter vertical acronym is not a usable search term where it collides with a Romance-language stem, and a pipeline that trusts `total_count` on such a head over-reports the vertical. **`esco mcp server` → `7` is really `2`.** The two genuine hits are `Dembelinho/ESCO-MCP-Server` (index `NONE`, not shelved) and `colaberry/WorldOfTaxonomy` (shelved below).

### 🔴 `T457` — the index hid a permissive grant on the one North America row

[`CredentialEngine/osmt`](https://github.com/CredentialEngine/osmt) is indexed **`NOASSERTION`**. The payload at `LICENSE` is `10,827 B`, sha256₁₂ `43508f2110ee`, and its first line is **`Copyright 2021 Open Skills Network`** followed by *"Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0"*. 🟢 **It is Apache-2.0 with the appendix completed** — which is exactly why the classifier refuses it: the customised header displaces the title block the index matches on. The standard Apache-2.0 payload read twice this same pass measures `11,352 B` (`CredentialRegistry`) and `11,358 B` (`transcript-ctid-matcher`); `osmt` is `525 B` shorter because the boilerplate appendix was replaced by a real notice.

🔴 **Consequence, and it is a commercial one:** the single most deployable artefact in the North America half of this register has spent `751` passes invisible to this base, and any pipeline that filters on the index value alone would have discarded it as unlicensed. 🟢 **It is permissive and Globant can build on it.**

🔴 **`T459` — the grant is not open source, and it is not merely absent.** The only licence file in the repository sits at **lowercase `license`** (`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md` and `COPYING` all `404`, `3 / 3` controls). `923 B`, sha256₁₂ `875c1bdc92d1`, indexed `NOASSERTION`, and it reads:

> *"Everything including text and images in this project are protected by the copyright laws. You are allowed to use this material for **personal use** but are **not allowed to use it for any other purpose** including publishing the images, the project files or the content … in any form either digital, non-digital, textual, graphical or written formats."* — `Copyright © 2017 - Present. Kamran Ahmed`

🔴 **Globant cannot use this in a client deliverable, a curriculum, a slide or a learning-path product.** It is the most-cited learning-roadmap asset in the industry and it is **all rights reserved, personal use only**. The permission that *is* granted is narrow and explicit: sharing *links*, and read-only GitHub forks made for contributing back.

🔵 **The instrument lesson, and it is the second of its kind in two passes.** `p751`'s `T441` extended the probe chain with `LICENSE.TXT` after a missing path would have published an `8,208★` repository as a false grant-absence. **This pass, `2` of the `3` most consequential payloads sat at non-canonical paths again** — `LICENSE.txt` on the `681-J` repository and lowercase **`license`** here. The chain must carry lowercase variants, and a pass that stops at `LICENSE`/`LICENSE.md` will publish a `371,756★` all-rights-reserved repository as "no grant found" — which reads to a client as *permissive by omission*. `T464`.

### 🔴 `T449` — the boundary that decides whether this register is regulated at all, and it depends on the implementation

Allocating a school place is **Annex III point 3(a)** of the EU AI Act — admission or assignment of natural persons to educational institutions — and therefore high-risk, with obligations now applying from **`2026-12-02`**… **and this is exactly where a studio must not guess.** The sources read this pass **disagree**: most place Annex III point 3 at **`2027-12-02`** following the Digital Omnibus on AI, **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (published in the Official Journal `2026-07-24`), while at least one argues point 3 **was not amended** by that regulation. 🔵 **This base does not pick a side on the date and neither should a proposal: verify against the Official Journal text before putting a deadline in a client document.**

🔴 **The substantive boundary is sharper than the date.** The AI Act regulates *AI systems*. A classical CP-SAT or MIP model is a system of rules defined entirely by natural persons, which is the category the Act's own definition is generally read to exclude — so **the same business function may sit inside or outside Annex III depending on how it is built**: a deferred-acceptance mechanism or a hand-written constraint model is plausibly out of scope, while an LLM that *infers* the constraints, or a learned ranker that orders applicants, is plausibly in. 🟢 **This is a genuine architectural lever and should be stated to clients as one** — but it is an open legal reading, not settled law, and it must be confirmed by counsel. ⚠️ It also cuts the other way: a studio that reaches for an LLM because it is faster to prototype may convert an unregulated scheduler into a high-risk system.

### 🔬 The instrument findings — read these before trusting any byte constant in this base

**`T439` — this base's licence-reading method under-counts every payload by one byte and hashes a truncated file.** Reading a payload through shell command substitution strips the trailing newline. Controlled on `google/or-tools`' `LICENSE`:

| Method | Bytes | sha256₁₂ |
|---|---|---|
| Byte-exact (`curl -o` to a file) | **`11,358`** | **`cfc7749b96f6`** |
| Command substitution (the method in use) | `11,357` | `58d1e17ffe51` |

🔴 **The failure mode is worse than an off-by-one.** `11,357 B` *is* the true size of the stock Apache-2.0 payload, so the defective read of `or-tools` **produced a false match to the stock constant** while the hash silently disagreed. Every Apache-2.0 size in this base written before this pass should be treated as `n` or `n+1` until re-read byte-exact. 🟢 **`p750`'s figure was right and is hereby corroborated:** the stock payload is `11,357 B` · sha256₁₂ **`c71d239df917`**, re-confirmed this pass on [`TimefoldAI/timefold-quickstarts`](https://github.com/TimefoldAI/timefold-quickstarts), a third independent repository.

**`T440` — `728-A`'s "exact constant" method holds for MIT and fails for Apache-2.0.** Five distinct payload hashes were read this pass in a `52`-byte band, and **two of them collide on byte count**:

| sha256₁₂ | Bytes | Shape | Seen on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `c71d239df917` | `11,357` | 🟢 **stock** | `timefold-quickstarts`, and `p750`'s `detoxify` / `granite-guardian` (byte-identical pair, re-confirmed) |
| `58d1e17ffe51` | **`11,357`** | stock **+ leading blank line, − trailing newline** | `UniTime/unitime` |
| `cfc7749b96f6` | `11,358` | stock **+ leading blank line** | `or-tools`, `timefold-solver`, `scipopt/scip` |
| `94d81cb282da` | `11,344` | variant | `cvxpy/cvxpy` |
| `df825c8e037a` | `11,396` | variant | `Cardinal-Operations/ORLM` |

🔴 **Two different payloads at `11,357 B`. Quote the hash, never the size.**

**`T441` — the documented probe chain misses `LICENSE.TXT`, and it cost an `8,208★` repository.** [`Project-OSRM/osrm-backend`](https://github.com/Project-OSRM/osrm-backend) returned **no grant at `8` probed paths** under the chain as written and would have been published as a `T302` honest negative. The grant is at **`LICENSE.TXT`** — uppercase extension — and `raw.githubusercontent.com` is case-sensitive: `LICENSE.txt` → `404`, `LICENSE.TXT` → `200`, **BSD-2-Clause**, `1,297 B`, sha256₁₂ `df207517bc08`. 🟢 **The chain used for the rest of this pass was corrected to `LICENSE, LICENSE.md, LICENSE.txt, LICENSE.TXT, COPYING, COPYING.txt, COPYING.md, LICENSE-APACHE, LICENSE.rst, LICENCE, license`** and every successor pass should use that ordering. This is the `724-I` case-sensitivity class, second instance.

**`T442` — the pointer-licence pattern recurs, and the second instance resolves to a *more* permissive grant, not a worse one.** [`valhalla/valhalla`](https://github.com/valhalla/valhalla) is indexed `NOASSERTION` and its `LICENSE.md` is **`44 B`**: `For license information see [here](COPYING)`. Resolved by a second fetch, `COPYING` is **MIT**, `1,135 B`, sha256₁₂ `8bdb14aebb00`. `p747` found this shape on `GEANT/CAT`, where it hid a *bespoke* licence; here it hides an ordinary MIT. 🔵 **The lesson is the procedure, not the direction: a payload under ~`200 B` is a pointer, and a pointer must be resolved before the cell is written.**

**`T448` — two pollution modes in this pass's discovery channel, both measured.** (i) `or-tools mcp server` sorted by stars returns `Dataojitori/nocturne_memory` at **`1,426★`** — a long-term-memory server with no optimisation content — ahead of every real row; the register's true top is `22★`. (ii) `student sectioning` returns **`2,970`** repositories by matching the two words independently; UniTime's actual student-sectioning solver is one repository. 🔴 **An unread star-sorted head in this register is `60×` wrong.**

**`T451` — `5` index corrections this pass, `5 / 5` toward a knowable grant, `0` in the refusing direction.** `MiniZinc/libminizinc` `NOASSERTION` → **MPL-2.0**; `Pyomo/pyomo` `NOASSERTION` → **BSD-3-Clause** (Sandia, `DE-NA0003525`, US Government retains certain rights); `coin-or/pulp` `NOASSERTION` → **MIT**; `jump-dev/JuMP.jl` `NOASSERTION` → **MPL-2.0**; `valhalla/valhalla` `NOASSERTION` → **MIT**. All five are on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) with payload bytes and hashes. 🔵 **`NOASSERTION` in this register means "the index could not parse a composite or pointer grant", not "there is no grant"** — the opposite of its meaning in `p746`'s `winterop-com` rows, where the payload was actively proprietary.

### 🔴 `T428` — the finding that decides the whole register: the best safety classifier is not open source, the most-starred permissive one is archived, and the maintained permissive ones are narrow

A wellbeing or safeguarding agent's load-bearing part is the classifier that decides *this message is a
disclosure of self-harm* or *this image must not be on a school device*. The supply has a shape, and it
is not the shape a studio would guess:

The index says `NOASSERTION`, which understates the problem. |

recipe to implement** — and the components that make it defensible (a record store, an intervention
engine, a subgroup audit) are on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) and
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md). See `P254`.

### 🔴 The instrument findings that govern this pass — read before trusting any address above

🔴 **`T417` — the URL-verification channel this register is specified to use is closed in this session.** `curl -sI https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>` returns **`403`** on `3 / 3` hosts probed; the agent proxy does not pass `github.com` HTML. **A `403` here is not a `404`** and must never be recorded as a dead address. The channel that does work is `gh api -X GET search/repositories -f q="repo:<owner>/<name>"`, where `total_count == 0` or HTTP `422` is the real "not a canonical current address" signal. Every address on this page was confirmed through that channel.

🔴 **`T418` — `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>` returns `403` for every repository outside this session's grant**, while `gh api search/repositories` answers for the *same* repository. Per-repo licence and star metadata must therefore be read through the **search** endpoint.

🔴 **`T422` — the two addresses the accessibility literature actually cites are both dead.** `zorkow/speech-rule-engine` → **`422`** and `NSoiffer/MathCAT` → **`422`**. Both moved to organisations: [`Speech-Rule-Engine/speech-rule-engine`](https://github.com/Speech-Rule-Engine/speech-rule-engine) and [`daisy/MathCAT`](https://github.com/daisy/MathCAT). A successor citing the author-named forms will write two `404`s.

it?** The answer is the federation layer — SAML metadata aggregation, protocol proxying, attribute
release and group/entitlement authority — and on this base it was **completely absent**:

| `Internet2/grouper`, `comanage-registry` | the entitlement and collaboration authority | **0** |

| `REFEDS`, `SeamlessAccess` | the standards and discovery layer | **0** |
| `casdoor` | `14,806★` Apache-2.0 **agent-first** IAM | **0** |

## 🆕 `p746` — 🟢 **THE EMIS AND OFFICIAL-STATISTICS AGENT TIER: `6` ROWS OVER A SUBSTRATE THIS BASE DID NOT HOLD AT ALL. 🔴 THE MOST COMPLETE TOOLKIT IN THE TIER IS INDEXED `NOASSERTION` AND IS PROPRIETARY ON READING THE BYTES.**

> **Pass p746 · every grant byte-read 2026-11-10 over the raw channel.** This pass changed the
> noun again — `p743` asked what a research university *runs*, `p744` what its *scholarly APIs*
> are, `p745` what the regulator *assesses* — and `p746` asks **what a national education
> ministry runs its numbers on**. 🟢 **The answer was a `252`-repository platform at `0` mentions.**

🟢 **Why this tier is commercially different from the three before it.** Tutoring agents sell to a
faculty; assessment agents sell to a registrar. **An EMIS agent sells to a ministry**, which is the
buyer with a procurement budget, a statutory reporting duty and a multi-year contract. The agent
layer over that substrate is `6` repositories deep, the best of them is `7★`, and **the substrate
underneath it is `BSD-3-Clause` and deployed in dozens of countries** — which is the combination
this base exists to find. `P231` is the recipe.

### The `6` rows

register this base has swept for `744` passes — has agents at tens of thousands of stars. Assessment
has agents at single digits. But it is **assessment**, not tutoring, that the EU lists in Annex III,
that Vietnam's `Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg` names as high-risk, and whose transparency duty is **live
now** under Article 50. **The open-source supply is absent exactly where the regulated demand is.**
That is a build case, not a shopping list, and `P226`–`P230` are what it buys.

> re-measures at **`4,568` trending addresses, `1,282` shelf addresses, `3,590` never promoted**
> across **`271` owners with `≥2` unpromoted repositories**. `27` addresses were promoted this pass,

> License", so a keyword test for the Affero licence matches plain `GPL-3.0`. Two of the rows below
> were initially filed `AGPL-3.0` and are **`GPL-3.0`** — the difference between a studio that may
> host a modified instance for a client and one that may not. The working discriminator is
> `AGPL-3.0`'s own section 13 heading, **`Remote Network Interaction`**, which `GPL-3.0` does not
> contain: `7 / 7` decided this pass.

> of its legs on publishers in the backlog and the result is the opposite kind of row: **not an

> Moodle.** That is a less exciting row and a far more sellable one.

| **EsupMyIA** | [EsupPortail/esup-myia](https://github.com/EsupPortail/esup-myia) | 10 | 1 | 🔴 **No grant in `HEAD`** — `16` licence paths probed, `16 / 16` → `404`; index `None`; no per-file header | **EMEA** | **The French higher-education consortium's reference stack for a sovereign ChatGPT-equivalent — and it is a specification, not a dependency.** The README is explicit: *"votre IA souveraine pour l'enseignement supérieur"*, first choice **ILaaS (CINES)** for inference — Mistral Medium 3 by default, plus Mistral Small, Llama, Qwen, Gemma and `bge-m3` embeddings — with **LiteLLM** as the gateway (quotas, load-balancing), **OpenWebUI** as the interface, PostgreSQL for logs/budgets/keys and Redis for caching; alternatives are an AMUE or Pro Mistral key. ### 🔴 `738-A` — five subjects refused, and the pattern in the refusals is the finding

> general LLM-methods work under MIT or Apache-2.0. Measured `4 / 4` against `5 / 5`.**
>
> `ECNU-ICALK` is the **East China Normal University** group behind `EduChat` (already held on this
> shelf). `p738` read the bytes of ten of its repositories across **14 probed licence filenames
> each**. The split is total and it falls exactly along the subject line:

| Subject | Repo | Stars | Grant at 14 probed paths |
|---|---|--:|---|
| Education | [ECNU-ICALK/AutoSkill](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/AutoSkill) | **613** | 🔴 none |
| Education | [ECNU-ICALK/ELL-StuLife](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/ELL-StuLife) | 73 | 🔴 none |
| Education | [ECNU-ICALK/EduChat-Math](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/EduChat-Math) | 60 | 🔴 none |
| Education | [ECNU-ICALK/SocraticMath](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/SocraticMath) | 18 | 🔴 none |
| LLM method | [ECNU-ICALK/EasyCL](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/EasyCL) | 28 | 🟢 Apache-2.0 (`11,324 B`) |
| LLM method | [ECNU-ICALK/SkillGym](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/SkillGym) | 17 | 🟢 MIT (`1,078 B`) |
| LLM method | [ECNU-ICALK/AutoMem](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/AutoMem) | 7 | 🟢 Apache-2.0 (`11,357 B`) |
| LLM method | [ECNU-ICALK/TextualRL](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/TextualRL) | 3 | 🟢 MIT (`1,078 B`) |
| LLM method | [ECNU-ICALK/DiscernMem](https://github.com/ECNU-ICALK/DiscernMem) | 1 | 🟢 MIT (`1,080 B`) |

> 🔴 **`AutoSkill` at `613★` is the single largest education-subject repository this pass
> enumerated and it cannot be used.** Nor can `SocraticMath`, which is the shape this base has
> repeatedly said it wants — Socratic rather than answer-giving maths tutoring.
> ⚠️ **`ECNU-ICALK/PsychEval` (`63★`) does carry a `19,346 B` licence, and it is a
> `CC-BY`-family *content* licence, not a software grant** — a distinction that matters because an
> evaluation corpus under CC-BY can be *cited* and *redistributed* but does not license the
> harness around it.
> 🟢 **Why this is worth publishing rather than just skipping five rows:** it is a reusable
> prediction. For a research group of this kind, **the grant tracks the artefact's audience, not
> the group's policy** — methods go out permissively because the group wants them cited and built
> on, and the education systems stay ungranted because they are tied to a product, a partner
> institution or a dataset the group does not own outright. So when a leg returns a high-star
> education repository from a university lab, **probe the grant before reading the README**, and
> expect the lab's *neighbouring* method repositories to be the usable ones. Denominators in
> `evidence/ecnu-grant-asymmetry.2026-11-10-p738.tsv`. ⚠️ `T302` still binds: the honest negative
> is *"no grant found at 14 probed paths"*, not *"no grant exists"*.

### 🔴 `738-B` — the keyword legs are not merely low-yield, they are matching a different word

> 🔴 **`0` education rows from `46` read, across two keyword legs, and the mechanism is
> substring capture: `tutor` matches inside `tutorial`, and `grading` matches inside
> `upgrading`.** This is a sharper statement than `T326`'s "prose legs produce none" — it names
> *why*, and it tells a later pass which words are unusable rather than which channel is.
>
> - `tutor created:>2026-09-01 stars:>30` → `47` total, `25` read, **`0` education**. ⚠️ **`16` of
>   the `25` are one operator**: `apimart*` throwaway accounts (`apimartat5`, `apimartmk8`,
>   `apimartjz`, `apimartu1n`, `apimarta6j`, …), every one `NOASSERTION`, every one named
>   `*-api-*-tutorial`, all in a uniform **`55`–`87★`** band, all created in the same window.
>   That is **organised star inflation on LLM-API-reseller promos**, and it matched this leg only
>   because `tutorial` contains `tutor`.
> - `grading created:>2026-09-01 stars:>20` → `81` total, `25` read, **`0` education**, top row
>   `2,580★`. The matches are `upgrading` inside the prose of generic video-skill and
>   motion-graphics repositories.
>
> 🟢 **Standing instruction, because it is cheap to obey:** a keyword leg in this industry must use
> a word that is **not a prefix of a common unrelated word**. `tutor`, `grading`, `student`,
> `education` and `curriculum` all failed this pass (`5` legs, `79` rows read, **`0`
> education rows**); `topic:` legs and `org:` legs did not fail this way at all, because neither
> matches prose. Full denominators in `evidence/substring-pollution.2026-11-10-p738.tsv`.

### 🔬 `738-C` — controls re-run, and two more channels are dead

> 🔬 **All of `p736`/`p737`'s instrument findings hold.** `gh api -X GET search/repositories`
> is **alive** and carried all `58` repository legs (`topic:`, `org:`, `user:` and
> keyword+`stars:` all `200`); the unauthenticated `curl https://api.github.com/search/repositories`
> fallback returns **byte-identical** metadata (`topic:ai-tutor` → `891` on both, `2 / 2`), so both
> channels work and `T310` stands. The ordered raw chain discriminated **`2 / 2`**
> (`openedx/edx-platform` `200`; an invented address `404`). `curl -sI https://github.com/{o}/{r}`
> → **`403` for the live repository too, `2 / 2`, still no discriminating power.**
>
> ⚠️ **`p738` names two new dead channels, and together they close the question of whether this
> base can ever enumerate a publisher's tree cheaply.** `T289` said the `repos/` namespace is dead
> under session repository-scoping. `p738` measures that the alternatives are too:
> **`api.github.com/orgs/{org}/repos` → `403`**, **`api.github.com/users/{user}/repos` → `403`**,
> and **`api.github.com/graphql` → `403` with an explicit body**: *"GitHub GraphQL is not
> available from Claude Code sessions; use the REST API."* 🔵 **Consequence, stated as a budget
> fact:** `search/repositories` is the *only* enumeration channel, it is capped at **`30`
> requests/minute** authenticated and **`10`** unauthenticated, and it returns at most `100` rows
> per call sorted by one key. A publisher leg therefore costs one request and sees only the head of
> that publisher's list — which is why `p738` read `25` rows per leg and recorded the
> `total_count` beside every one (`evidence/leg-ledger.2026-11-10-p738.tsv`), so a later pass can
> see what it did *not* see.

> were aimed at this shelf — `topic:intelligent-tutoring-system`, `topic:ai-tutor` (with a

> star floors. 🔴 **`9` of the `12` returned rows this base already holds, and `12 / 12` produced
> zero fileable rows.** On the four legs with the strongest filters the result was total:
> `topic:education-ai stars:>15` → **`3 / 3` held**; `topic:mcp education stars:>10` → **`7 / 7`
> held**; `topic:ai-tutor stars:>25 pushed:>2026-10-15` → **`7 / 7` held**; `topic:intelligent-
> tutoring-system` → **`6 / 6` held**. Three legs returned **`0` results at all**
> (`AI teaching assistant stars:>150`, `lesson plan generator AI stars:>60`,
> `plataforma educativa IA stars:>20`). Full denominators in
> `evidence/leg-ledger.2026-11-10-p737.tsv`.
>
> 🟢 **Read positively, this is a statement about the shelf's condition rather than about the
> pass: the English-language AI-education agent namespace is saturated against this base, and
> `p736`'s `7` rows plus `p735`'s were the tail of it.** The novel rows that remain carry
> **`≤ 6★`, no grant, or both** — `topic:ai-grading` returned `4` novel addresses whose maximum was
> `6★`; the one novel row on `rubric grading LLM stars:>25` has no licence and was last pushed in
> 2024. ⚠️ **`T316` therefore generalises beyond recency:** it is not only the recency head of a
> saturated topic that is worthless, it is the *whole* of a saturated topic once this base has
> swept it. Spend the legs on publishers instead (`T326`).
>
> 🔴 **And the most natural explanation for the regional gaps was tested and refuted — see
> `T323`.** If this shelf is `0` for EMEA-authored learning agents (`T290`) and thin for LATAM, the
> obvious hypothesis is that discovery is being run in the wrong language. `p737` ran **`10`
> non-English legs** — `lernplattform`, `nachhilfe`, `tuteur intelligent`,
> `plateforme pédagogique IA`, `tutor IA educación`, `asistente educativo IA`,
> `tutor inteligente IA estudiantes`, `plataforma educacional IA`, `corretor redação IA`,
> `plataforma educativa IA` — returning **`45` novel repositories whose maximum was `18★`, with
> `0` fileable.** The best Iberian-language rows the channel returns are **already held**
> (`matheussilvano/mooose`, Apache-2.0, ENEM essay marking; `LabSirius/TutorIA`, MIT, Colombian
> rural higher education). **The regional shortage is a supply fact, not a discovery-language
> artefact**, and no later pass should spend legs re-testing it.
>
> 🟢 **Where `p737`'s budget went instead, and why it was the right call:** to the publisher tier,
> which produced **`14` fileable rows** — `10` on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)
> and `4` on [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md), plus a corrected gate. The
> single most consequential of them is a pair: **`openHPI/codeocean` (BSD-3-Clause) and
> `openHPI/poseidon` (MIT) — a permissive, production-proven sandbox that executes student code.**

> blocking delivery was never an agent (`P192`, `737-A`).
>
> 🔬 **Controls re-run, and both of `p736`'s instrument findings hold.** `gh api -X GET
> search/repositories` is **alive** and carried all `53` repository legs of this pass (`T310`
> confirmed, `topic:` / `org:` / `user:` / keyword+`stars:` all returning `200`); the ordered raw
> chain discriminated `2 / 2` (`openedx/edx-platform` `200`, an invented address `404` on all
> probed names). ⚠️ **`p737` records one new dead channel, and it bounds what any pass here can
> verify:** `WebFetch` against arbitrary external hosts fails at DNS resolution — `2 / 2`
> (`www.i-scoop.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`) — because this session's network policy

## 🆕 `p736` — reads and verifies `2026-11-10`

> **`7` rows added below, every one read from the bytes today, and `1` high-star subject refused on
> its grant.** 🟢 **The headline of this pass is an instrument retraction, and later passes must read
> it before they trust `T298`: the supply channel `p735` declared dead is alive and was used for
> this entire pass.** `gh api -X GET search/repositories` returned `200` with complete metadata for
> **`topic:`, `org:`, `user:`, `language:` and `stars:` queries** — `topic:ai-education` → `581`,
> `topic:ai-tutor` → `891`, `user:mitodl` → `186`, `org:apereo` → `30`. ⚠️ **It still returns `422`
> for exactly one query shape: `repo:{owner}/{name}` — and that is because `repo:` is not a valid
> qualifier for the repository-search endpoint at all, not because the credential failed.** `p735`
> tested the channel with that one query, read the `422` as an outage and migrated the base off a
> working instrument. `gh auth status` *does* still report `GH_TOKEN` invalid — so the credential
> warning is **not predictive of channel health** and must not be used as one (`T310`).
>
> 🔴 **Second instrument finding, and this one is a defect in a gate this pass wrote itself.** A
> licence gate that substring-matches family names **over the whole payload** misclassifies **every
> GPL-3.0 repository as AGPL-3.0**, because the canonical GPL-3.0 text names the AGPL three times in
> **§13, "Use with the GNU Affero General Public License"**. Measured on `2 / 2` GPL payloads
> (`h5p/h5p-php-library`, `ging/escapp`) before it was caught; both are **GPL-3.0**, not AGPL. The
> repair and its controls are published as **`P188`** and the full truth table as
> `evidence/licence-gate-controls.2026-11-10-p736.tsv` — **`25 / 25` payloads classified correctly
> under the repaired rule**, including `4` the GitHub index gets wrong and `3` the base's own
> LICENSE-file probe would have scored as grant-absent (`T311`, `T312`).
>
> 🔵 **Why this pass reports raw bytes and a clause verdict instead of the published
> `1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f` and `9942 B`/`3fda30957805` digests.** The `11` MIT payloads read today
> span **`1,060 B` – `2,285 B`** raw, and one valid **Apache-2.0** grant
> (`apereo/java-cas-client`) is a **`2,321 B` short-form notice that is not the canonical Apache
> text at all** — so no size band, and no digest of a canonical text, can decide it. The operative
> clause can, and did, `25 / 25` (`T313`). Rows below therefore state the licence file, its raw
> size and the clause that decided it.
>
> 🔬 **Controls re-run, all four, none changed a verdict.** `curl -sI https://github.com/{o}/{r}`
> → `403` for a repository proven alive **and** for an invented address, **`2 / 2`, still no
> discriminating power**. The ordered raw chain discriminated the same pair **`2 / 2`**
> (`200` for `openedx/edx-platform` at `README.rst` — third in the chain, so the order is still
> load-bearing; `404` on all five filenames for the invented address).
> `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` → `403`, and **`p736` can finally name the cause: the error body
> says so** — *"GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session. Use add_repo to
> request access."* `T289`'s dead `repos/` namespace is **session repository-scoping**, not a
> GitHub or network property, which is why no pass here can enumerate a tree (`T302` stands).
> 🔵 **And the Open edX index gap holds `1 / 1`:** `edx-platform in:name` returns forks only
> (`Edraak` `13★`, `microsoftarchive` `5★`) while the raw channel returns `200` — the absent star
> count on [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) is still correctly recorded as absent.

This one personalises to the **teacher**.** The asset it produces is an institution's own pedagogical practice, captured as an artefact that outlives the individual — which is exactly the thing a university cannot buy and a consultancy cannot supply. 🔵 **Why that is commercially sharp:** it converts the retiring-professor problem and the adjunct-churn problem into a data-capture engagement, and the deliverable is owned by the institution rather than rented from a vendor. 🔵 It is also **harness-agnostic by construction** — no application to deploy, which is the `T227` agent-native posture this base keeps arguing for. ⚠️ Chinese-primary README (an `README.en.md` exists); requires the source teacher's material, so it is useless without institutional consent to use it. 🟢 Region placed on a Chinese-primary corpus. |

Astro + strict TypeScript, bilingual English / 简体中文. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,090 B` raw, `Permission is hereby granted, free of charge` → **MIT**; index agrees. 🟢 **Filed because it answers the one objection that kills AI tutoring pilots in STEM subjects: a generated paragraph is not an explanation of a mechanism.** This base holds tutors that converse (`DeepTutor`), tutors that quiz, and tutors that mark — it held nothing that *shows how a thing works and then lets the student change it*. 🔵 The "explore the model after watching it" loop is the cheapest demonstrable learning-gain artefact in a physics, chemistry or engineering engagement, and it is MIT. ⚠️ Content-authoring effort is the real cost — the repository is the renderer and the interaction layer, not a library of finished visualisations. 🟢 Region placed on a bilingual Chinese-primary corpus. |

Gate: `LICENSE`, `34,523 B` raw, title line `GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` → **AGPL-3.0**; index agrees. 🔴 **Filed with a licence warning, not as a build-on candidate.** AGPL-3.0's network clause is triggered by offering the software as a service — which is how a studio would deploy this — so **Globant cannot build a hosted product on it** without releasing the derived source. 🟢 **It is filed anyway because the *architecture* is the finding and architecture is not licensed:** `scan → human approval → execute`, with the recurring pattern held as learned state, is the right shape for every "agent acts inside the client's LMS" engagement, and it is the shape an auditor will accept. Read it, do not fork it. 🔵 Pairs conceptually with the held `boyuchengz/canvas-mcp` (MIT, read-only) — the permissive route is MCP for access plus your own workflow layer. ⚠️ Region unplaced. |

### 🔴 `736-R` — one subject refused, and it is the fourth-largest repository this pass enumerated

[`walkinglabs/hands-on-modern-rl`](https://github.com/walkinglabs/hands-on-modern-rl) — **`4,886★`**,
pushed `2026-10-23`, an open hands-on curriculum carrying learners from basic reinforcement learning
to LLM alignment — is **not filed, and will not be filed**. The GitHub index reports `NOASSERTION`.
The payload is a **`519 B` `LICENSE` whose first line reads
`Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 4.0 International`.**

🔴 **`NonCommercial` is disqualifying, not merely restrictive.** Globant's use of anything on these
shelves is commercial by definition, and `ShareAlike` would additionally reach derived teaching
material. This is the first row this base has refused on a **NonCommercial** term rather than on a
copyleft or an absence, and it is recorded here because **`4,886★` and "open-source curriculum" in
the description is exactly the combination that gets a repository into a client deck unread**.
🔵 Generalisable: a `NOASSERTION` index value means *read the bytes* (`T301`) — and sometimes the
bytes say no.

### 🟡 `736-S` — the agent shelf's discovery channel is clean, and its recency head is worthless

`topic:ai-tutor` (`891` repositories) and `topic:ai-education` (`581`) are the two **cleanest** legs
of this pass: sorted by stars they return this base's own held rows, which is the correct behaviour
for a saturated namespace. 🔴 **Sorted by recency they return almost nothing usable: of the `20`
most recently pushed `topic:ai-tutor` repositories, `20 / 20` carry `≤ 7★` and `17 / 20` carry
`≤ 2★`**, all pushed `2026-11-09` or `2026-11-10`. The three rows this pass took from the fresh head
(`selfsemester`, `vistep`, and `teacher-to-skills` from the conjunctive leg) were found on
**stars-and-push** jointly, never on recency alone. 🔵 **Stated as a standing instruction because it
is a cost finding: do not spend a leg on the recency head of a saturated topic.** Full denominators
in `evidence/leg-pollution.2026-11-10-p736.tsv` (`16` legs, `4` measured nils, `6` pollution modes).

## 🆕 `p734` — reads and verifies `2026-11-10`

> **`7` rows added below.** Every grant was read from the bytes and put through **`P180`**, the gate
> published this pass. `P180` is `p733`'s `P176` with **one missing step supplied** and **its scope
> narrowed to the two families it can actually certify**. The missing step is a *trailing-space
> strip*: `tr '\n' ' '` turns the payload's final newline into a trailing space, so `P176` run
> exactly as written lands **one byte over** its own published constant on every payload. With the
> strip, **`6 / 6` MIT payloads read this pass gate to `1020 B` / `fe2a9817987f`** — the constant
> `p733` published — across raw sizes `1,061`–`1,076 B`. See `T286`.
>
> 🟢 **The Apache constant `p733` declared unreproducible (`T280`) is reproducible, and this pass
> computes it: `9942 B` / `3fda30957805`.** Reproduced on **`5` independent payloads across `5`
> publishers and `3` filenames** (`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENSE.md`), raw `11,356`–`11,412 B`.
> `p733`'s `9130 B` / `6e1ffb7fc4e2` is not reproducible from any rule tested here and is retired
> by `T287`. The Apache row below carries the new constant.
>
> ⚠️ **The gate is not applied to BSD at all this pass, and that is a correction rather than an
> omission.** Three BSD payloads produced three different values; the cause is measured (`T288`)
> and it is not the holder name. BSD rows on this base's pages are filed with their measured bytes
> and **explicitly not certified**.

## 🆕 `p732` — reads and verifies `2026-11-10`

> **`9` rows added below.** Every grant was read from the bytes and put through a **repaired gate
> published this pass as `P172`/`P173`** — not the line rule this base has used since `p728`.
> 🔴 **The reason is a defect in that line rule, demonstrated under control this pass (`T272`):**
> it drops *every* line containing the string `copyright`, which in a stock MIT payload is **three**
> lines — the holder attribution, **the attribution obligation itself** (`The above copyright notice
> and this permission notice shall be included in all`), and **part of the liability disclaimer**
> (`AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER`). The `870 B` /
> `3d87852cf34a` constant this base has certified MIT rows against for many passes is therefore
> computed over a text **with two substantive terms deleted**, and it is holder-invariant *because*
> of that deletion.
>
> 🟢 **Repaired rule (Channel H): drop only the holder line, keep every term.** New MIT constant
> **`1020 B` / `fe2a9817987f`**, measured identical across **`18 / 18`** MIT payloads read this pass
> whose holder lines range `24`–`53` bytes and include one containing CJK
> (`Copyright (c) 2026 speechlab0210 (蝦說 AI / 小金)`). Holder-invariance is preserved without
> deleting anything that binds.
>
> 🔬 **Controls (7, published so the claim is checkable).** Channel L **passes at the exact stock
> constant** a payload whose attribution obligation has been negated (`shall be included` →
> `need NOT be included`) and one whose disclaimer has been inverted (`BE LIABLE` → `ARE FULLY
> LIABLE`), because both tampers stay inside lines it discards. Channel H flags both
> (`1023 B`/`8487e8205c74`, `1027 B`/`01eb22566b08`). ⚠️ **Honest limit, stated rather than hidden:**
> a restriction *appended to the holder line* (`… Jason Tangen. NONCOMMERCIAL USE ONLY.`) is
> invisible to **both** channels, since both discard that line. Holder-invariance and
> holder-line-tamper-detection cannot both come from one hash, so the gate adds a **separate
> holder-line shape assertion** and that control now flags. `7 / 7` controls behave as specified;
> `18 / 18` real payloads pass.

## 🆕 `p730` — twenty subjects verified, eighteen filed, and six of the seven largest carry a grant Globant cannot build on

**`18` new repositories filed (`8` agents · `6` platforms · `4` foundations) · `6` found, verified and
NOT filed on licence grounds · `3` new trends (`T262`–`T264`) · `2` new patterns (`P166`, `P167`) ·
`4` new regulatory instruments placed by region · `2` gaps declared from legs that ran and returned
nothing.** Discovery ran through `gh api -X GET search/repositories` with `topic:`, date-bounded and
star-bounded legs — `18` queries, every subject's grant read **from the bytes** at the default ref.

| `p730` registered | Verdict |
|---|---|
| 🔴 `730-A` → `T262` — **the licence trap is concentrated in the high-star education repositories, and the index field does not say so.** Of the `20` subjects verified this pass, **`6` of the `7` largest by stars cannot be built on**: [`cn-math-taxonomy`](https://github.com/xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy) (`7,244★`, **CC BY-NC 4.0** — non-commercial), [`StudyOnTV`](https://github.com/chadyi/StudyOnTV) (`1,159★`, *"Source Available License — Personal Use Only"*, © *all rights reserved*), [`education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills) (`969★`, **CC BY-SA 4.0** — content share-alike), [`advisingapp`](https://github.com/canyongbs/advisingapp) (`340★`, **Elastic License 2.0** — not OSI, no hosted-service use), [`knowledge-graph`](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph) (`167★`, licence resolved **per dataset**, not per repository), [`Agent4Edu`](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/Agent4Edu) (`102★`, AAAI 2025 oral, **no grant file at any ladder path**). 🔴 **`search/repositories` reported `NOASSERTION` for five of the six and `NONE` for the sixth — it flagged them "unknown", never "unusable".** A pass that treated `NOASSERTION` as "probably fine, check later" would have filed all six | 🔴 **`6` REFUSED, EACH REASON ON RECORD** |
| 🟢 `730-B` — **the permissive education-ERP gap recorded at `724-C` is narrower than this base thought.** [`yungifez/skuul`](https://github.com/yungifez/skuul) — **`477★`, MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `b0fe88bfb0d2`, © *2022 Ebuka Ifezue*), pushed `2026-11-01` — is a **multi-school** management system under a permissive grant. 🔵 Every SIS/ERP row this shelf holds is copyleft or vendor-licensed: `openeducat_erp` (LGPL-3.0), `i-educar` (GPL-2.0), `rosariosis` (GPL-2.0), `frappe/education` (unresolved), `advisingapp` (Elastic 2.0, refused above). **Multi-tenancy matters more than the star count**: a studio that can host many schools on one deployment is selling a platform, not an installation | 🟢 **GAP NARROWED, NOT CLOSED** |
| 🟢 `730-C` — **two licence payload constants extended, and one of them was already in this base's own records.** **AGPL-3.0** at `34,523 B` / sha256₁₂ `0d96a4ff68ad` now reads `n=3` byte-identical: `p728`'s `lector` row plus this pass's [`gygy-open/question-bank`](https://github.com/gygy-open/question-bank) and [`JudgePeach/math-question-bank`](https://github.com/JudgePeach/math-question-bank). **MPL-2.0 opens at `n=2`, spread `0 B`**: `16,725 B` / sha256₁₂ `1f256ecad192` for both [`rolling-scopes/rsschool-app`](https://github.com/rolling-scopes/rsschool-app) and [`edrys-org/edrys`](https://github.com/edrys-org/edrys). 🔵 And `codelion/adaptive-classifier`'s Apache payload (`11,357 B` / `c71d239df917`) is **byte-identical to `p714`'s `mwalimulens-agent`** — a cross-pass match this base can only see because it prints the bytes | 🟢 **`n=3` AGPL · `n=2` MPL, BOTH `0 B` SPREAD** |
| 🟢 `730-D` → `T263` — **four jurisdictions mandate school AI curriculum in the same school year, and each one names its delivery channel.** China's **AI + Education Action Plan** (Ministry of Education plus four departments, April 2026) with Beijing's **≥ 8 class-hours per academic year**; India's **CBSE Computational Thinking & AI for Classes 3–8**, launched `2026-04-01` for the `2026-27` session under circular `Acad-15/2026`, framework by an **IIT Madras**-chaired expert committee, **~500,000 teachers** trained through the **NISHTHA** platform; **Saudi Arabia**'s `50` AI learning units across `22` updated subjects for **6M+** students (**SDAIA** + Ministry of Education + National Centre for Curriculum + MCIT); the **UAE** Cabinet's AI curriculum for **all** public and private schools, **22,000** teachers trained, with **MBZUAI** and the Emirates College for Advanced Education on design and **Code.org** advising on content. 🔵 **The demand this creates is teacher training and curriculum localisation at national scale — not another tutoring app** | 🟢 **FOUR INSTRUMENTS, THREE REGIONS** |
| 🔴 `730-E` → `T264` — **the forecast spread widened again while actual capital formation fell.** Three `2026` base-year estimates: **`$9.58B`** (Precedence, `34.52%` CAGR to 2035), **`$10.6B`** (Research and Markets, `41.5%` to `$42.48B` by 2030), **`$11.40B`** (Coherent, `16.8%` to `$57.25B` by 2033). 🔴 **A `2.5×` spread in CAGR on the same base year is not a forecast, it is three different definitions of the market.** Against it, HolonIQ counts **`$1.0B` of edtech venture capital in H1 2026, `−26%` year on year**, after `$2.4B` in full-year 2024 — **`−89%` from the `$20.8B` 2021 peak**. 🔵 **Forecast and funding point in opposite directions, and for a studio that resolves cleanly: the money is in budget-funded institutional work** — ministries, districts, university systems — **not in venture-funded product work** | 🔴 **SPREAD WIDER, CAPITAL DOWN** |
| 🟡 `730-F` — **two gaps declared from legs that ran.** (1) **Africa is unmeasured.** The Middle East / Africa leg ran and returned **Gulf states only** — Saudi Arabia and the UAE, in detail — and **nothing** on any African ministry, curriculum or player. This base's `EMEA` coverage is Europe plus the Gulf; **the African third of `EMEA` is a declared hole, not coverage.** (2) **India's delivery vendors are unknown.** The CBSE instrument, its committee, its circular and its teacher-training channel are all documented; **which vendors or partners actually deliver it is not** — the leg returned curriculum policy and no procurement. 🔵 Recorded because an informed gap is usable and silence is not | 🟡 **DECLARED, NOT INFERRED** |
| 🔴 `730-G` — **two published positions this pass contradicts, and the contradiction is in Globant's favour.** `p729` recorded the four mandated global supply legs as a **36th consecutive nil** and the regional legs as **saturated**. Run as `gh api` search legs instead of as prose queries, the supply side returned **`20` live, novel, education-shaped subjects in one pass, `18` filable** — so the nil was an instrument artefact, not an absence of supply. 🟡 **The regional legs were two-thirds right:** market *figures* are indeed saturated (every CAGR and adoption number this pass surfaced was already on the page), but **four regulatory instruments and two 2026 funding series were not.** 🔵 **Re-scope, do not retire: run the regional legs for instruments and capital, not for market size** | 🟡 **NIL WAS THE INSTRUMENT; SATURATION IS PARTIAL** |

**Filed:** `18` rows. **`20` subjects probed, `20` alive (`README` → `200` on the raw channel), `18`
filed, `6` refused on licence** (four of the refusals are also published as rows in
`verticals/solutions.md` § *Not open source*, so a later pass cannot re-file them from a search result).
Every grant read from **bytes** at the default ref over the case-ordered ladder
(`LICENSE` → `LICENSE.md` → `LICENSE.txt` → `LICENCE` → `COPYING` → `UNLICENSE`); `0` licence claims
taken from `spdx_id` alone. `curl -sI https://github.com/…` **not used** — it returns `403` through
this proxy for every address, live or invented, confirmed again this pass.

**Gates, re-derived by counting after the inserts:** agents shelf **`56/56`** at `7` columns, agents
roster **`353/353`**, foundations roster **`369/369`**, verticals roster **`134/134`** — `0` ragged
rows, `0` header/placeholder/example rows surviving as data, `0` duplicated slugs. Region vocabulary
**`56/56` legal** against the five permitted values, `0` illegal.

## 🆕 `p729` — the licence gate published at `p728` cannot be run, and the band it replaced is biased against institutions

**`9` new agent rows (shelf `39` → `48`) · `10` more rows across the other two shelves · the stock-`MIT`
constant re-derived WITH its procedure after `7` normalisations failed to reproduce the published one ·
the raw band shown to overshoot on `3` institutional publishers and `0` others · an `Apache-2.0`
constant established at `n=8` · the shelf's largest row found shipping a NON-CANONICAL `Apache-2.0`
payload · a third provenance class for `spdx_id` found and one row refused because of it · the mandated
supply legs nil a `36`th time and the regional legs saturate as a market instrument · `4` new trends
(`T258`–`T261`) · `2` new patterns (`P164`, `P165`).**

| `p729` registered | Verdict |
|---|---|
| 🔴 `729-A` → `T258` — **`p728` published a constant and not the procedure that produces it, so `P163` — a gate it declared standing and mandatory — is not executable.** The published value is `1,020 B` / sha256₁₂ `fe2a9817987f` for the normalised stock-`MIT` body. **Seven normalisations were tried against publishers `p728` itself names as stock `MIT`** (keep newlines / drop blank lines / rstrip lines / ±trailing newline / strip ends / collapse all whitespace) and **every one lands in `870`–`874 B`. None reaches `1,020 B`.** 🔴 **And the reason this is not a rounding quarrel:** two of the seven are each perfectly stable across publishers and **disagree with each other** — whitespace-collapsed is `870 B` / `3d87852cf34a`, newline-preserving is `870 B` / `a7a3589acf19`. **Same byte count, different hash.** So a gate that records only the length cannot detect that it is running a different procedure from the one that calibrated it. **A constant without its normalisation is not a measurement.** `P164` republishes the gate with the procedure written out and both numbers attached | 🔴 **GATE NON-EXECUTABLE AS PUBLISHED; REPLACED** |
| 🔴 `729-B` → `T259` — **the raw byte band this base used for two years overshoots on institutional publishers, and only on them.** Three overshoots are now on record against the published `1,056`–`1,097 B` stock-`MIT` band, and **all three carry an institution in the copyright line**: `SirhanMacx/gnps-civic-readiness` **`1,112 B`** (© *Great Neck Public Schools — Social Studies Department*, with a `3`-byte em-dash), `ilios/frontend` **`1,105 B`** (© *The Regents of the University of California*), and `p726`'s `opencast/studio` **`1,164 B`** (a European HE consortium, two copyright lines). **`0` individual-author payloads overshot in any pass.** All three normalise to `870 B` / `3d87852cf34a` — they are stock `MIT`, and the band was wrong about all three. 🔴 **The consequence is not cosmetic: the band is biased against school districts, university systems and public consortia — the exact supply tier whose absence this base declared as North America's nil at `703-G` and never re-opened.** The instrument was suppressing the region | 🔴 **BAND RETIRED — IT WAS SELECTING AGAINST THE MISSING REGION** |
| 🟢 `729-C` → `T258` — **the gate generalises past `MIT`, which `p728` asserted but did not measure.** `Apache-2.0`, normalised by `P164`'s procedure: **`9,478 B` / `399d47608b81` for `8` independent publishers, spread `0 B`** (`SocialCoach`, `teacher-to-skills`, `agentfactory-manufacturing`, `lore`, `ZAFU-Paper-Formatter-Skill`, `tutorial-creator`, `numbas/editor`, `gaokao-vault`) against a **raw** spread of `34 B` (`11,324`–`11,358 B`). 🔵 **First data points for two further tiers, recorded as `n=1` and explicitly NOT yet constants:** `BSD-3-Clause` `1,091 B` / `90bd78b45663` (`freeCodeCampOS`) and `Unlicense` `1,042 B` / `fe0829b13da5` (`ebisu.js`, grant at `UNLICENSE` — a ladder path this base's probe did not hold until this pass) | 🟢 **`n=8` CONSTANT; TWO TIERS OPENED HONESTLY** |
| 🔴 `729-D` → `T260` — **the one `Apache-2.0` outlier is the largest row on this shelf, and the defect is in the licence text itself.** [`HKUDS/DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) (**`57,571★`**) normalises to **`9,494 B` / `fe1d4bc9501f`**, `+16 B` against the `n=8` constant. Diffed line-for-line against `apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0.txt` fetched this pass: the **APPENDIX application instructions are rewritten**, with *"Apache"* replaced by *"Alarm"* — *"Please also get an `\"Alarm or alarm\"` copyright header … (Alarm is the umbrella trademark for a collection of Open Source projects)"* — plus two wording changes in operative text (§1 `Contribution`: *"received by **the** Licensor"*; §4(d): *"excluding **any** notices"*). 🟢 **The verdict is NOT to refuse the row.** The grant, patent and warranty sections are byte-intact; the APPENDIX is instructions for *applying* the licence, not licence terms, and the two §-level edits are an article and a synonym. **It is substantively `Apache-2.0` and stays on the shelf.** 🔴 **But two instruments both fail on it in opposite directions:** an exact-payload gate **rejects a `57,571★` row that is correctly licensed**, and GitHub's `spdx_id` — which reports a clean `Apache-2.0` — **never flags that the text was altered at all.** Hence `P164`'s rule: **verify by section, not by whole-payload equality** | 🟡 **ROW KEPT; BOTH GATES WRONG ON IT** |

The field's source is `package.json`'s `"license": "MIT"` — read this pass. **A package-manifest field is a declaration by the author; a grant is a document.** This base's standing rule is that a licence is read from bytes, so the row is **found, verified alive, and deliberately NOT filed**, with the reason recorded rather than the row quietly dropped. 🔵 `spdx_id` now has three known provenances — grant file, manifest field, absent — and only the first is admissible here | 🔴 **ROW REFUSED, REASON RECORDED** |

## 🆕 `p728` — the stock-`MIT` byte band becomes an exact constant, and the shelf is 39 rows

**`10` new rows · `29 / 29` shelf rows re-measured (a FULL re-read, denominator stated per `T223`) · `5` star cells corrected in the row · `3` new trends (`T255`–`T257`) · `2` new patterns (`P162`, `P163`) · `1` instrument upgraded from a tolerance band to an exact constant with `4` negative controls · the mandated legs nil a `35`th time · `2` channel-pollution modes measured and named.**

### 🟢 `728-A` — `726-A`'s proposed remedy is correct, and it resolves to a single constant: `1,020 B`

`726-A` found that this base's stock-`MIT` **raw** byte band (`1,056`–`1,097 B`, `41 B` spread) has two
benign false-positive modes — a decorative underline plus a second copyright line
(`opencast/studio`, `1,164 B`), and **multi-byte UTF-8 in the copyright holder's own name**
(`zzllrr/mather`, `1,098 B`), the second of which penalises every non-ASCII publisher by
construction. It *proposed* a remedy — apply the band to the grant body with the title block
and every `Copyright` line stripped — but did not measure one.

**Measured this pass on `12` independent publishers, with whitespace normalised** (which also
removes the hard-wrap-versus-reflow variable that raw byte counts cannot see):

| | Raw payload | Normalised grant body |
|---|---|---|
| Band across `12` publishers | `1,063`–`1,164 B` | **`1,020`–`1,020 B`** |
| Spread | `101 B` | **`0 B`** |
| sha256₁₂ | `12` distinct values | **`fe2a9817987f`, identical for all `12`** |

The `12` include **both** of `726-A`'s documented false positives (`opencast/studio` and
`b310-digital/teammapper`, each carrying **two** copyright lines) and **two** non-ASCII
holders (`zzllrr/mather`; `132465798a/zx-study`, © *2026 正贤*). **Every one normalises to
`1,020 B` exactly.** Both false-positive modes disappear, and the non-ASCII penalty disappears
with them.

🔵 **Negative controls, because a test that accepts everything is not a test:**

| Control | Normalised body | Verdict |
|---|--:|---|
| `Apache-2.0` payload (`opencast/editor`) | `10,056 B` | 🟢 rejected |
| `AGPL-3.0` payload (`heuwels/lector`) | `33,449 B` | 🟢 rejected |
| `MIT` **+ an added non-commercial clause** (synthetic) | `1,098 B` (`+78`) | 🟢 **caught** |
| `MIT` **with the warranty disclaimer deleted** (synthetic) | `559 B` (`−461`) | 🟢 **caught** |

🟢 **The instrument is now an equality test against one constant, not a tolerance band:**
a payload is stock `MIT` iff its normalised grant body is `1,020 B` with sha256₁₂
`fe2a9817987f`. 🔴 **And note what the old band could not do:** `mather`'s benign raw `1,098 B`
and the tampered payload's malignant normalised `1,098 B` are the *same number*. The raw band
cannot separate them; the constant separates them cleanly. Filed as `T255`, wired into `P163`.

### 🟢 `728-B` — the ten rows, and the two categories the shelf did not have

Seven of the ten are education-native tutors, authoring tools or study platforms. The two that
matter most for an engagement are the ones that are **neither**: [`malgaroshy-maker/factoryforge`](https://github.com/malgaroshy-maker/factoryforge)
(`41★`, MIT — PLC programming taught against a Godot physics factory) and
[`DNiev/ecu-lab`](https://github.com/DNiev/ecu-lab) (`67★`, MIT — engine calibration taught
against a simulated dyno). 🔵 **Both are vocational / TVET, which this shelf had `0` rows of in
`727` passes**, and both implement the same shape: *a high-fidelity simulator of a rig too
expensive to put in front of a learner.* That is a different buyer (industrial training budget),
a different success measure (certification pass rate) and no LMS to sit beside. Filed as `T256`
and `P162`.

  keyword-stuffed titles** of the form *"Best Open-Source X for 2026"* / *"X 2026: Interactive Y"*,

  repository cannot be built on regardless**, so the cost here is reviewer time, not legal risk —
  but the cluster is large enough to crowd a ranked result page. Filed as `T257`.

## 🆕 `p727` — the conjunctive `topic:` leg pays where eighteen single heads nilled, and the shelf is 29 rows

**`3` new rows · `14 / 29` shelf rows re-measured (a partial re-read, and the denominator is stated because `T223` requires it) · `4` star cells corrected · `2` new trends (`T253`, `T254`) · `1` new pattern (`P161`) · `1` stale prose count fixed · the mandated legs nil a `34`th time · `4` controls re-run, `0` changed verdict.**

### 🟢 `727-A` — the `topic:` channel is not unusable; a *single* `topic:` head is

`p726` measured the `topic:` channel hard and filed the verdict in `726-F`: **`18` heads, `1,013`
distinct repositories, `600` novel (`59.2 %`), `0` filed** — killed by homonymy, with
`topic:adaptive-learning` returning concept-drift ML and `topic:xapi` returning Cisco Webex
device code. **That measurement stands. Its generalisation does not.**

This pass ran the same channel **conjunctively** and it produced every one of this pass's
three rows:

| Leg | Shape | Novel rows filed |
|---|---|--:|
| `topic:education topic:llm pushed:>2026-09-01 stars:>100` | two heads, ANDed | **3** |
| `topic:ai-tutor pushed:>2026-09-01` | one domain-bearing head | 0 (all `12` already held) |
| `topic:intelligent-tutoring-system pushed:>2026-06-01` | one domain-bearing head | 0 (all already held) |
| `grading agent LLM rubric pushed:>2026-09-01 stars:>50` | phrase leg | 0 rows returned at all |

🔵 **Mechanism, and it is `726-D`'s rule arriving on a second channel.** `726-D`/`T252`
established that *a single-term leg needs a domain-bearing term*, because `maestro` is a
conductor and an orchestrator. A `topic:` head has exactly the same defect —
`adaptive-learning` is a pedagogy and a sampling strategy, `xapi` is the Experience API and a
Cisco endpoint — and **conjunction is the fix, because it supplies the domain-bearing term as
a second filter the author had to opt into.** `topic:education topic:llm` cannot return
concept-drift detection: nobody tags a gradient-boosting library `education`.

🟢 **Rule (`T253`): run the `topic:` channel as a domain head ANDed with a technique head, never
as a single head.** The single-head form is what `726-F` measured, and at `0 / 600` it should
not be run again.

### 🟢 `727-B` — the three rows, and two of them are copyleft

[`DilawarShafiq/nietzschian-debugger`](https://github.com/DilawarShafiq/nietzschian-debugger)
(**`555★`, MIT**) is the find worth the pass. It is a debugger that **withholds the answer**
and returns the question — productive struggle implemented as a refusal rather than a tone.
🔵 This shelf holds many agents that *explain*; this is the first that is built not to.
**And it creates a measurement problem the shelf has not had**: an agent whose correct output
is the absence of an answer cannot be graded by comparing its answer to anything, which is
why `P161` wires it to an objective judge rather than to a rubric.

[`sitor-ai/frametell`](https://github.com/sitor-ai/frametell) (**`490★`, AGPL-3.0**, `96` forks
in one month) and [`helixnow/deep-student`](https://github.com/helixnow/deep-student)
(**`309★`, AGPL-3.0**, `60` forks) are both **local-first desktop learner applications with
maintained Simplified-Chinese translations** — and both are copyleft. That pairing is not a
coincidence and is filed as `T254`.

## 🆕 `p726` — the shelf is 26 rows, and the discovery instruments were measured rather than obeyed

**`26` education-native agents, every one verified `2026-11-09`.** `24` rows were inherited,
**`26 / 26` were found in the index, `0` archived, and no licence changed.** `2` rows are new.
The count is derived by counting data rows between this section's table header separator and
the blank line that terminates the table, *after* the insert — not carried from the previous
pass's sentence, which is how `p703` published `199` over a table of `233`.

`26 / 26` rows resolved in `search/repositories`; `0` archived; **no SPDX value changed on any row.**

| Row | Published | Read `2026-11-09` | Δ |

| `DeepTutor` | 57,511 | **57,526** | +15 |

Twenty-one rows were unchanged to the star. Every delta is in single or double digits, which is the
