### 🟡 `726-K` — a row filed one pass ago has not been pushed in six and a half months

[`isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP`](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) was filed at `p725` as the
find that closed the EMEA foundational gap. Its `pushed_at` is **`2026-04-22`** — `201` days before the pass
that filed it, and the cell was published without a currency marker while this shelf flags
`learn-agentic-ai` for the same condition. 🔵 **The defect is not the row, it is that enthusiasm and
currency were assessed by different instruments in the same pass**: the licence and the capability were
read first-hand, and the date was copied without being compared to anything. ⚠️ Marked `⚠️` in the cell.
It remains the only open Skolverket-API client found and is still the right starting point — but it is a
**single-author artefact at `13★` that has not moved in two quarters**, and that belongs in the cell a
studio reads, not in a paragraph it may not.

### 🔴 `726-D` — `T249`'s single-term remedy trades a false nil for a homonymy false-positive rate, and this pass measured it

`725-D` established that multi-word non-English legs return categorical zeros because
`search/repositories` ANDs every term against name, description and topics only. The remedy filed as
`T249` was **single terms plus a `stars:` floor.** Run across **`30` single-term legs** at `stars:>=15`
this pass, the remedy works — and it imports a failure the phrase legs could not have:

| Leg | Top novel permissive rows it returned | Education? |
|---|---|---|
| `maestro` | `mobile-dev-inc/Maestro` (16,364★ mobile E2E test runner), `Netflix/maestro` (3,852★ workflow orchestrator), `roboflow/maestro` (2,695★ VLM fine-tuning) | 🔴 **None** |
| `guru` | `redis-developer/ArXivChatGuru`, `InderKumarRathore/DeviceGuru` (iOS device detection), `iansinnott/alfred-maestro` | 🔴 **None** |
| `aula` | `brunocampos01/banco-de-dados` (425★ — a Brazilian database **course's own notes**) | 🟡 Coursework |

🔵 **The mechanism is that a single term maximises recall by discarding the only disambiguation the
channel had.** `maestro` is a conductor *and* an orchestrator; `guru` is a teacher *and* a brand suffix.
This is the `p646` homonymy finding — *"the week's top novel address is a brightness daemon"* —
reproduced on the **language** channel rather than the `topic:` channel, which makes it a property of
the instrument and not of either vocabulary. 🟢 **Rule, filed as `T252`: a single-term leg needs a
domain term, not a common noun.** `skola`, `escola`, `sekolah`, `教学` are domain-bearing; `maestro`,
`guru`, `aula` are not, and a leg built on one should be read as unranked rather than as supply.

### 🔴 `726-E` — and the deeper result: the language channel's modal object is coursework, not courseware

Across the sixteen education-bearing language legs, the novel rows are overwhelmingly **students' own
repositories**: exam archives (`ExamenesViejos-FAMAF-Computacion`, `111milprogramadores/examenes`),
42-school project dumps (`ALL_SCHOOL_42`, `COMPLETED_PISCINE_C`, `Projets_42_SP`), course companions
(six separate `Escola Talking About Testing` Cypress-course repos), and lecture notes
(`nad-garraz/algebraUno`, a LaTeX algebra text by students at UBA Exactas). **Of roughly forty-five
education-native novel rows, two are deployable education systems** —
[`resultsystems/school`](https://github.com/resultsystems/school) (195★, MIT, **Brazil**, unpushed since
`2021-12-28`) and [`ikhsan3adi/sistem-perpustakaan-qr-code`](https://github.com/ikhsan3adi/sistem-perpustakaan-qr-code)
(92★, GPL-3.0, Indonesia). 🔵 **This is why the language channel felt rich and filed thin for eight
passes**: the noun that names a school in any language is used mostly by people *attending* one. 🟢
**Consequence for later passes: the language channel is a good instrument for placing a region and a
bad one for finding a platform.** The platform channel, measured below, is the publisher estate.

### 🟢 `726-B` and `726-C` — what actually paid: two estates, one of them a national government

Both new rows on this shelf, and all eight filed on the sibling shelves, came from **owner
enumeration of education-native publishers** — not from any phrase, language or topic leg. The two
new agent rows came from the language sweep's domain-bearing legs (`教育`, `教学`); everything of
platform scale came from estates. See `repos/foundations.md` and `verticals/solutions.md` for
`eGovFramework` (Korea) and `opencast`, and `agents/trending.md` for the measured yields.

## 🆕 `p725` — the shelf is 24 rows, and four of p724's own cells were stale

**`24` education-native agents, every one verified `2026-11-09`.** That is the real count — no
header rows, no example rows and no padding to reach an arbitrary minimum. `23` rows were
inherited and all `23` were found in the index with **`0` archived**; `1` was promoted from the
roster.

### 🔴 `725-A` — p724 measured four rows, wrote the new values in prose, and left the table cells stale

p724's own narrative below records `DeepTutor` **57,462 → 57,493**, `OpenMAIC` **43,124 → 43,131**,
`Study-Mate` **1,402 → 1,404** and `moodle-ai-skill-navigator` **279 → 278**. **None of those four
numbers was ever written into the table cell.** The table published the pre-measurement values
while the prose two screens below reported the post-measurement ones.

🔵 **The compiler reads the cell, not the paragraph.** A measurement that exists only in prose has
not been published — it has been *mentioned*. The four cells are corrected in this pass's re-read,
and the rule is filed as `P158`: **a pass may not report a re-measurement it did not write into the
data row.** This is the second defect of its family in two passes (`724-T` published a data row as a
table header; this one publishes a stale cell under fresh prose) and both share a cause — the shelf
table gets treated as narrative rather than as the data product.

### 🟢 `725-B` — the re-read, and what a star cell actually is

`23 / 23` inherited rows present in the index, `0` archived, **no licence changed**, and the largest
true delta against the **cells** was `DeepTutor` **57,462 → 57,509** (`+47`); against p724's prose
figure it is `+16`. Other movement: `OpenMAIC` **+9**, `OATutor` **278 → 279**, `SocialCoach`
**473 → 474**, `moodle-ai-skill-navigator` back to **279**, and eighteen rows unchanged to the star.
`panaversity/learn-agentic-ai` remains stale at `2025-10-26` — now **over twelve months** without a
push, and still correctly flagged as courseware rather than live code.

⚠️ **`DeepTutor` was read twice during this pass, roughly 25 minutes apart: `57,509`, then `57,511`.**
A star cell is therefore a *timestamped reading*, not a fact, and a small disagreement between two
passes on a fast-moving repository is not evidence of the `722-C` inflation failure. The test that
distinguishes them is magnitude: `722-C` was wrong by factors — claiming `~12k` for a `387★`
repository — not by twos.

### 🟢 `725-C` — the jurisdictional standards layer has arrived as open artefacts, and both are EMEA-authored

[`isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP`](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) (`13★`, **MIT**,
TypeScript, Sweden) is promoted to the shelf above, and
[`YannHY/scenarisation`](https://github.com/YannHY/scenarisation) (`7★`, CC-BY-SA-4.0, PHP,
France/Switzerland) is filed on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md). Together they
are the first instruments this base has found for the gap `T203` named — *the standards layer, not
the model, is the chokepoint between a demo and a delivery*:

- **Skolverket-MCP** exposes a *ministry's own* curriculum, school-unit register and
  planned-education data as MCP tools. Curriculum alignment stops being a scraping problem.
- **scenarisation** encodes the vocabulary: eight competency frameworks (Socle commun,
  **DigComp 3.0**, **GreenComp**, CRCN, Pix, **Pix IA**, PER romand, Florimont), Bloom's taxonomy,
  and — the part that matters most — **the AIAS AI Assessment Scale as a first-class field on every
  activity**, with school-system referentials for France, Switzerland, the United States, the three
  Belgian communities, England, Wales, Scotland, Northern Ireland, the European Schools, the IB and
  ISCED 2011.

🔵 `T213` recorded that *"every region's 2026 regulation converges on one control, and it is the
control open education tooling was missing entirely."* **That control is "at what level is AI
permitted on this task", and it is now shipped, open and multi-jurisdictional.** Both artefacts are
tiny and single-author, and neither is a platform: they are **vocabulary and access**, which is
precisely what the engagements this base describes keep rebuilding by hand. Recipe filed as `P157`.

### 🔴 `725-D` — this base's non-English discovery legs have been returning a syntax artefact, not a regional nil

Five multi-term non-English legs were run first and returned **`0` rows each**:
`educación IA profesores` · `educação IA professores` · `escuela colegio docente IA` ·
`Schule Lehrer KI Unterricht` · `école enseignant IA pédagogie`. The *same languages as single
terms*, with a `stars:` floor, returned real repositories by the dozen — `educacao` →
[`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) `724★` and
`Universidade-Livre/ciencia-da-computacao` `21,023★`; `docentes` → eight rows; `scuola` →
`iisgiua/giuaschool`; `skola` → **`Skolverket-MCP`**; `enseignant` → **`scenarisation`**.

🔵 **Cause:** `search/repositories` **ANDs** every term and matches only name, description and
topics — **not the README**. A three- or four-word non-English query therefore requires every one of
those words in a single short description, which essentially never happens. **Both of this pass's new
finds came from single-term legs, and neither was reachable by any phrase leg.** Remedy, filed as
`T249`: run non-English regional legs as **single terms plus a `stars:` floor**, never as phrases —
and treat any leg returning categorical zero as an instrument failure to retest, not an absence to
write down. This is the measured mechanism behind `T234` (*a declared regional nil is more often a
fact about the base than about the region*) and `T215` (*the search language was the regional
ceiling*): the ceiling is narrower than "language" — it is **term count**.

(`DeepTutor` **57,462 → 57,493**; `OpenMAIC` **43,124 → 43,131**; `Study-Mate` **1,402 → 1,404**;
`moodle-ai-skill-navigator` **279 → 278**, a real decrease; fourteen rows unchanged to the star).

### 🟢 `724-K` — the new rows are a category, not four unrelated finds

Three of the four read a *recording* and return something a learner can study from, and two of
them — `course2md` (`508★`, Rust) and `lecture-to-notes` (`117★`, Python) — were built
independently, three weeks apart, in different languages, against the identical problem: **the
lecture recording is the artefact every institution already has and none of them can search.**
`course2md` optimises for reach; `lecture-to-notes` optimises for data boundary, and its
ordering is the one that matters for `T229` engagements — retrieval, OCR and ASR local,
generation last and constrained to assembled evidence. Where recordings cannot leave the
premises, it is the only one of the two that is deployable at all, and it is MIT.

### 🔴 `724-I` — this file's own stated probe has a second false-negative mode

The note at the head of this file describes existence-checking against
`raw.githubusercontent.com/{owner}/{repo}/HEAD/README.md`, *"falling back to `README.rst`"*.
**`course2md` returns `404` on both** — its file is lowercase `readme.md`. Full `P142` rung 1
reaches `LICENSE` third and gets `200`, so the **existence verdict is still correct**; the loss
is the **payload**, which `723-R` made the replacement instrument for region attribution after
`gh api users/{owner}` went `403`. A pass following the procedure literally places `course2md`
in **no region at all**. The chain is corrected in `compose/patterns.md` (`P142`): lowercase
`readme.md` goes between `README.md` and `README.rst`, **ordered, never either/or** — controls
re-run this pass show `DeepTutor` and `OpenMAIC` at `README.md` `200` / `readme.md` `404`.

### 🟡 `724-L` — a licence split that is not a wrong index field

[`jangviktor-web/GongkaoNaoku`](https://github.com/jangviktor-web/GongkaoNaoku) (`100★`) is filed
on `repos/foundations.md` rather than here, and with a split verdict: its `LICENSE` payload was
read and **is genuine Apache-2.0 text — the index is correct** — while its README badge reads
`许可-仅供个人学习` over **27,506 real 行测 civil-service exam questions**. Every prior licence
finding in this base corrected a *field*. This one separates **code from content inside a
repository whose field is accurate**: the spaced-repetition engine is reusable, the corpus is
not. Stage the engine, never the bank.


### 🔴 `723-U` — p722's shelf was accurate and unrepresentative at the same time

p722 published nine rows and wrote *"that is the real count."* The nine were correct. **The
sentence was not**, and the evidence was already inside this file: the 345-row roster below
held permissive, actively-maintained, education-native repositories that were **larger than
seven of the nine shelf rows** and had simply never been re-measured — `Study-Mate` (1,402★),
`anki-mcp-server` (551★), `SocialCoach` (473★), `teacher-to-skills` (472★),
`universal-examprep-skill` (372★), `Bloom` (302★), `ari` (217★), `InterviewMentor` (116★),
`Mentingo` (109★). All nine were re-verified today and promoted; one further repository
(`adhd-study-coach`, 94★) is new to this base.

🔵 **The failure mode is specific and worth naming: a "verified-this-pass" shelf silently
becomes a shelf of *whatever the pass happened to look at*.** p722 measured the rows it
inherited, found four of them overstated, and never asked whether the roster held better
rows. A curated shelf must be re-selected from the whole roster each pass, not re-measured
in place — filed as `P149` in [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).

### Region attribution is evidence-based, and five rows could not be placed

`gh api users/{owner}` — the obvious way to place a repository by its author — returns
**`403` in this scoped session** (*"sessions are bound to their configured repositories"*),
for every one of eleven owners queried. It joins `curl -sI github.com/...` on the list of
instruments that do not work here. Regions above were therefore read from **payload
evidence**: README script and country words, verified this pass — `Study-Mate` (2,384 Han
characters), `SocialCoach` (2,671 Han + "China"), `teacher-to-skills` (2,406 Han + "China"),
`Mentingo` (Selleo's registered address, Bielsko-Biała, Poland, per CB Insights and
Startup-House directory entries).

🟡 `anki-mcp-server`, `Bloom`, `ari`, `InterviewMentor` and `adhd-study-coach` carry **no
regional signal in their payloads at all** and the owner endpoint is closed. They are filed
**`Global`** because that is the honest value, **not because they are global projects**. A
later pass with a working owner endpoint should re-place them; until then, do not count them
as evidence of supply in any region.

## General agent frameworks used as education substrate

These are not education products; they are the runtimes the rows above are built on or
most often composed with. Included because an engagement needs the substrate named.

| Framework | Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Note for education use |
|---|---|--:|---|---|---|
| AutoGen | [microsoft/autogen](https://github.com/microsoft/autogen) | 61,761 | **MIT (code)** | 2026-10-28 | Multi-agent conversation; the usual choice for teacher/student/grader role simulation. ⚠️ Licence nuance below. |
| smolagents | [huggingface/smolagents](https://github.com/huggingface/smolagents) | 30,280 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-11-03 | Code-writing agents in a few hundred lines; the lightest base for a tutoring loop you must audit. |

### Licence nuance — AutoGen is dual-licensed and the index reports the wrong one

`search/repositories` returns `CC-BY-4.0` for `microsoft/autogen`, because the repository
root `LICENSE` is the Creative Commons text covering documentation. The **code** licence
is at `LICENSE-CODE` and is **MIT** (read this pass: `MIT License / Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation`).
Globant can build on the code under MIT. A pass that copied the index value would have
recorded AutoGen as un-buildable-on and dropped it. Filed as a standing caution: for any
Microsoft repository, read `LICENSE-CODE` before trusting the index's SPDX field.

## Regional supply — what each leg actually returned

The four regional legs ran. Their yields are genuinely asymmetric, and the nils are
measured, not silent.

- **APAC — richest supply by a wide margin.** Both top agents (DeepTutor, OpenMAIC) are
  Chinese academic projects, and the national-exam vocabulary leg (`高考`/gaokao) is
  productive: `OpenLMLab/GAOKAO-Bench` (806★, Apache-2.0), `deekur/gaokaomath` (1,946★,
  CC-BY-4.0, 1952–2026 exam corpus), `ziqihe10-droid/xuefeng-agent` (909★, AI exam-choice
  advisor), `alchaincyf/zhangxuefeng-skill` (10,726★, MIT).
- **North America — non-zero this pass, breaking a four-pass "zero" record.** The previous
  four passes filed nothing for NA. Two real US academic projects were filed today:
  **OATutor** (UC Berkeley, MIT) and **Submitty** (Rensselaer, BSD-3-Clause — on the
  foundations shelf). The earlier nil was a search-vocabulary artefact, not an absence of
  US supply: US authors do not tag by country, so country-shaped queries miss them, while
  *institution-shaped* queries (lab names, university orgs) find them.
- **EMEA — measured nil on supply, rich on regulation.** The national-exam leg
  (`Abitur`/`baccalauréat`/`Lernplattform`) returned nothing filable: the best result is a
  34★ static revision site. One EMEA-authored agent is on the shelf (`tutor-mcp`, 99★),
  found through a non-regional query. **EMEA's value in this industry is policy, not
  repositories** — see `intel/market.md` for the EU AI Act timeline.
- **LATAM — thin but real, via exam vocabulary not language.** Searching Spanish
  education words (`educación`, `educativo`) is a **dead leg**: it returns teaching-demo
  clones (TikTok clones, landing pages "para fines educativos"), not education technology.
  Searching the *national exam* works: `yunger7/enem-api` (366★, GPL-2.0),
  `gustavoguanabara/assistente-enem` (121★, GPL-3.0), `piresramon/gpt-4-enem` (54★, MIT,
  LLM evaluation on Brazil's ENEM). Recorded so the next pass drops the language leg and
  keeps the exam leg.

## Licence posture for Globant

Build-on-able today (MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD / ECL): DeepTutor, OpenMAIC, educhain, OATutor,
OpenTutor, tutor-mcp, smolagents, AutoGen (code), Submitty, Kolibri, GAOKAO-Bench.

Copyleft — usable but check the deployment model before customising for a client:
moodle-ai-skill-navigator (GPL-3.0), Moodle (GPL-3.0), Open edX (AGPL-3.0), Canvas (AGPL-3.0).
AGPL matters specifically because an LMS is normally *offered as a service*, which triggers
the network clause.

Not open source despite appearances — **do not** stage these as a starting point:
`CaviraOSS/PageLM` (custom "PageLM Community License"), `weponusa/teachany` (custom dual
licence). Both read as OSS in listings; neither is. Verified by reading `LICENSE.md`/`LICENSE`
this pass.

Absent entirely: **§7 Disclaimer of Warranty, §8 Limitation of Liability, §9 Accepting Warranty, and the APPENDIX.** It ends `Copyright 2026 StudyForge Contributors`. 🔴 **So the permissions survive and the liability cap does not** — the single worst asymmetry a licence file can have for the party *redistributing* it. GitHub reports `NOASSERTION`, which is correct and which a licence field read from the API alone would have surfaced as a shrug rather than as this. 🔴 **Do not ship it to a client; mine the psychometrics and reimplement, or get the grant fixed upstream first.** 🔴 **Region REFUSED:** no locative token in the `main` README. |

| **dsh-answer-reviewer** | [`bycall/dsh-answer-reviewer`](https://github.com/bycall/dsh-answer-reviewer) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,089 B`, sha256₁₂ `4bfa22e76f64`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2026 dsh-answer-reviewer contributors”*) | **10** | Global | 🆕 **New at p705 at `10★`, filed for its control loop rather than its adoption — it is a rubric gate, and this shelf has wanted one in a shippable form for several passes.** A host plugin that intercepts *every* turn before it closes: it extracts the assistant's final user-facing text, hands it to a **dedicated reviewer model** scored `1`–`100`, and if the score is under a configured `threshold` (default `80`) it steers the agent back into the **same turn** carrying the reviewer's single most important finding as concrete feedback. After `maxChallenges` steers (default `5`) the reply closes as-is — and the README is explicit that **the cap is a leak guard, not the gate; the score threshold is the gate**. 🟢 Two properties make it reusable as *assessment* infrastructure rather than just agent polish: the reviewer sees **every real user prompt** of the session (tagged `<all_user_prompts>`) with plugin-injected context — file notices, `AGENTS.md`, skill text, system reminders — **filtered out**, so it scores against what the learner actually asked; and threshold, on/off, cap and reviewer model are all re-tunable live with no host restart (config at `~/.dsh/answer-reviewer.json`, overridable via `REVIEWER_CONFIG_PATH`, plus a JSON API). Swap the generic quality rubric for a marking rubric and this is a re-gradeable formative-feedback loop |

The author states plainly that the framework does not guarantee grading quality, only that a bad run is visible within two minutes. Traditional Chinese first, with an English summary. |

This is the row to cite the next time the LATAM column is read as an absence of supply. 🔴 **And its grant is SCAFFOLDING BOILERPLATE, which is a second finding:** the `LICENSE` holder line reads *“Copyright (c) 2015-present 650 Industries, Inc. (aka Expo)”* — this is `create-expo-app`'s own template file, never replaced. The MIT text is real and the permission is effective, but **the named holder is the tooling vendor rather than the author**, so nothing in the repository actually grants the author's own work. Worth knowing before building on any freshly scaffolded project. |
